Known gap for the lead: model-gateway's build also writes resourceDomains origins into script-src and style-src, which this allowlist refuses, so with both switches on only connect-src-only grants take effect until the two sides agree. | - Deviations: Notice: the 'needs network' notice is hidden when the validated policy names at least one origin, whether all or part of the need is granted. The spec names the notice only for the fallback cases; keeping it over a granted origin would state something false. A partly granted app is not told which origin is missing. The alternative (hide only when connect-src grants) is equivalent for model-gateway's output today, because any granted resource origin also lands in script-src and fails validation.; sandboxWidgetDocument re-runs validatedAppCsp on the csp it is passed and falls back to PREVIEW_CSP on failure, so no caller can place an unvalidated policy in srcdoc. The frame also calls validatedAppCsp once to decide the notice; the rebuilt string validates to itself.; validatedAppCsp requires the same directive names in the same order as PREVIEW_CSP (exact match; empty parts dropped) and rebuilds its output from the accepted sources. connect-src takes origins in place of 'none' because model-gateway writes it that way; the other three append origins after data: and blob:.; PREVIEW_CSP is now exported from sandbox-html.ts so the frame can tell a granted policy from the no-network one.; networkOrigins is read onto McpAppResource, but no code reads it; the frame decides from the policy only, as the spec's validation signature implies. |

The decline path uses Decision::split() and SpaceDenial::details_value(), so the private denial module did not need to change.; Error codes the spec did not name, taken from sibling routes: 403 active_membership_required when there is no org, 400 invalid_space, invalid_run_id, invalid_process_id and invalid_cursor (Model Gateway's id and cursor codes), and 503 delegated_auth_unavailable when the sandbox bearer cannot be minted. The SPA must render chunk text as plain text: the ledger treats process output as unscreened. |
| W4-P4 | F-t3-035 'turn ChatLiveRunPanel into a computer panel that picks a viewer by tool kind' (audit :838-848); EP-1 W4 | built | ChatLiveRunPanel now takes computerCalls and spaceRef. When VITE_CHAT_COMPUTER_PANEL is on and the turn is not ZDR, a tab strip sits above the browser region: Browser always, Terminal and Files only when the run used a terminal/process or file call. Terminal draws the latest such call through TerminalViewer, which for a process call follows the output via the new readRunProcessOutput client (404/403 returns null) every 2 s while the process is alive, appends chunk text as plain text, pauses while the tab is hidden, and stops on exit, unmount or repeated failure; with no Space ref it stays transcript-only. Files draws the latest file call through FileViewer. ChatPage passes the active turn's tool calls and the thread's Space. No new action contract and no new flag: chat.open_computer_viewer already exists and is unchanged, so the commit should say so. Code only: not compiled, linted or tested. The client assumes W4-P3's route (processes.rs) is registered; I did not see that registration in domains/chat.rs when I read the tree. | - Deviations: W4-P3 relays Model Gateway's body unchanged, so next_cursor and has_more sit under meta, not data as the spec's normalization line reads. The client reads meta first and falls back to data.; ComputerPanelTabs is a controlled tab strip plus the Terminal and Files viewers. The browser region stays in ChatLiveRunPanel and renders when Browser is selected; it is not drawn by ComputerPanelTabs. Switching tabs unmounts the browser region, so BrowserChrome's local state resets on return.; No strip is drawn unless the run used a terminal/process or file call, so a browser-only run keeps the panel it had. Browser is the default tab.; Process states are read as Model Gateway names them (uppercase). STARTING counts as live with RUNNING, since the spec says 'running'. EXITED, KILLED, EXPIRED and LOST end the tail. Any other state (UNSPECIFIED) shows 'unavailable' rather than 'ended'.; Hidden tab: polling pauses while document.visibilityState is hidden and resumes when visible, so the spec's 'stops on hidden tab' is read as a pause. Terminal and Files also unmount while the panel is hidden (props.hidden) or collapsed, which stops their polling.; readRunProcessOutput takes an optional fifth argument, an AbortSignal that is aborted when the tail stops. The first four arguments match the spec.; The client applies the same local dev-bypass Authorization header that requestJson applies. That is a duplicated one-liner, because applyDevAuth is not exported from http.ts.; The transcript path of TerminalViewer now renders the text of a process_read's chunks instead of the raw JSON envelope. This is outside the spec. It is on the flag-gated P1 path, which the tool cards also use.; The Space ref passed is activeProjectRef() (the thread's own Space), not projectRef(). projectRef() is gated by VITE_CHAT_PROJECTS_ENABLED, which would silently disable the live tail for Space threads when projects are off.; The live text is capped at 262144 characters (oldest dropped, with a note), gap_before shows a note, and the view follows its end unless the reader has scrolled up.; computer-viewer.css gained rules for the strip and a bounded live box. This file is not in the spec's list. The strip reuses the existing .verevon-chat-tabs and .verevon-chat-tab classes. |
| W4-SH-1 | F-t3-046 import track (audit :968-978); EP-12-S15 built SC + MG `POST /v1/threads/import` only, no BFF/SPA | built | Built the BFF proxy and SPA entry for importing a thread export. The gateway now has POST /api/v1/chat/threads/import (session-required, 2 MiB body cap) that relays the file to Model Gateway under the owner's bearers, as the export proxy does, and returns Model Gateway's answer and typed refusals unchanged; nothing is stored in the BFF. The SPA adds the thread.import action contract beside thread.export (with its registry entry, output schema and model-access row), importThread() in chat-client.ts returning {threadId, importedMessages, dropped}, and ImportConversationAction.tsx mounted beside export and fork on the latest answer behind VITE_THREAD_IMPORT (off). The control hides for temporary chats, Space threads and zero-retention callers, shows the two counts with a button that opens the new thread, and shows the server's refusal. Both gateway files pass a rustfmt syntax check; nothing was compiled or tested, per the code-only rule. | - Deviations: Model Gateway's POST /v1/threads/import reads the export file as the raw request body and the title from the `title` query parameter, not from a JSON {export_json, title} body. The BFF accepts {export_json, title} JSON from the SPA as the spec describes, then sends export_json as the raw body and a non-empty trimmed title as the query parameter. Forwarding the JSON object would have been refused as an invalid file.; The BFF attaches the owner's model bearer as well as the session bearer and x-user-id/x-org-id, reusing the export proxy's owner_export_request builder, so the import authenticates the same way the export does.; The BFF rejects a title longer than 96 characters with a typed invalid_request (not in the spec), because the title rides on the upstream query string.; The control is mounted in ChatMessages.tsx beside ExportConversationAction and ForkAction on the latest answer, the same condition as export. The spec's 'chat history menu' was read as that per-answer row, since export and fork live there. The sidebar ChatSessionMenu (CoreSidebar.tsx) has neither entry, so the control is not reachable without an open thread that has an answer.; After a successful import the control shows the counts and an 'Open the conversation' button that calls selectChatThread(threadId), rather than opening the new thread automatically. Opening it would unmount the control and lose the counts. The lead may change this if auto-open is preferred.; Files beyond the spec's list, all required: action-registry.ts (threadImportOutput, the thread.import descriptor, and the threadImportInput import), model-access.ts (the MODEL_ACCESS map is Record<ActionId, ...>, so the new id needs a row: exempt no_owner_executor, context_write), vite-env.d.ts (typed flag), ChatMessages.tsx (mount), and ImportConversationAction.css (styles, new).; The 2 MiB BFF body cap applies to the JSON envelope, which escapes the file text, so a file close to 2 MiB can be refused by the BFF with a plain 413 before Model Gateway's own check. The SPA maps a 413 to the 'larger than an import takes' message. |
| W4-P6 | F-t3-035 'output spill in the same family' / 'long output spilled to workspace files' (audit :838-848) | built | W4-P6 is built. process_read takes an optional spill flag that is honoured only when EXECUTION_PROCESS_OUTPUT_SPILL is the literal true (default off, compose line and flag-registry entry added). A spill read that finds more than one 256 KiB page after after_seq pages the registry through the same Space-checked call up to MAX_TOTAL_OUTPUT_BYTES, writes the output to <workspace>/.tmp/process-output/<process_id>.log, and returns spilled_to, bytes, next_cursor, has_more, state and the first 32 KiB inline. ZDR and waived runs are refused as feature_not_supported process_output_spill before any registry call or write, process_id must be a plain id before any path is built, and symlinked path components are refused. Nothing was compiled or tested (CODE ONLY); rustfmt --check shows no diffs in the changed hunks, and the remaining diffs in those files predate this change. Lead step: regenerate the ZDR catalogue golden (see not_done). | Lead step, required before mp-contracts is green: the ZDR catalogue changed (process_output_spill added to FEATURES), so regenerate it with MP_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 cargo test -p mp-contracts zdr_refusal and sync the copies: apps/Model Plane/api/contracts/zdr-refusals.json (+ .sha256), apps/Model Plane/go/pkg/zdrrefusal/zdr-refusals.json (+ .sha256), apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/api/contracts/zdr-refusals.json (+ .sha256); scripts/tests/zdr-contract-sync.test.sh checks the copies. Not regenerated here. The prompt-tool-manifest and do_tool_catalogue goldens should not move, because the process_read ToolDefinition block text is unchanged.; Flagged, not changed (predates W4-P6): with EXECUTION_CORE_SPACE_WORKSPACE_WRITE_THROUGH unset (the default), record_workspace_overlay runs the unfiltered upload at lease release, so code_interpreter scratch (.tmp, .mplconfig), attachments, the staged program and oversized files are uploaded into the Space. Only the spill directory is excluded from that branch.; Flagged, not changed: process_read stays classed read-only in the permission tables, but a spill writes a file into the workspace .tmp. The authority is cap.process.background, which already lets processes write there, so nothing new is granted. Decide whether spill calls should be classed as writes.; Not closed: create_spill checks each path component for a symlink and then creates the file. A process running in the same workspace could swap a directory for a link between the check and the create. This is the same race remove_staged_dir documents. Deviations: Spec says .tmp/ is excluded from CAS sync, so the spill is never durable. That holds only on the syncable paths. The default lease release (write-through off) uses the unfiltered diff_and_upload, which would have uploaded the spill. Adapted: sandbox_lease.rs record_workspace_overlay now calls diff_and_upload_where with a keep closure that skips .tmp/process-output (process_tools::is_output_spill). diff_and_upload_where in workspace_hydrate.rs is now pub(crate). No other upload changed.; process_output_spill is not in the spec's file list, but Refusal::feature_not_supported debug-asserts that its key is in zdr_refusal::FEATURES, so the key was added there. This makes the zdr-refusals golden stale (see not_done).; Workspace read and grep refuse or skip files over 1 MiB (workspace_edit::MAX_FILE_BYTES), so a spill above 1 MiB cannot be read with them. The spill still goes up to MAX_TOTAL_OUTPUT_BYTES as specified. The spilled_to note tells the reader to use code_interpreter (same tree) or to page with process_read when the file is over 1 MiB.; A spill-requested read on a switch-on, non-ZDR run resolves the Space workspace through ensure_space_workspace before reading, so it can acquire and hydrate a lease even when the output would fit in one page. A resolution failure is reported only when a spill is actually needed. The spec says reads do not acquire leases, so this is the one exception.; The spill schema is offered only when the switch is on. It is substituted in process_tool_defs after the list is built, not by editing the process_read ToolDefinition block, so the block text that prompt-tool-manifest pins is unchanged. Dispatch in runtime_loop/mod.rs and the offer in runtime_loop/agent.rs are outside the spec's file list but required.; Each spill replaces the earlier <process_id>.log, and the note tells the model to read it first. The spec was silent on this.; Spill text maps NUL bytes to U+FFFD and decodes each chunk lossily, so the workspace tools accept the file. A multi-byte character cut by the host's 64 KiB forced line split shows as two replacement characters.; 'More retained output than MAX_READ_BYTES after after_seq' is implemented as has_more on a page read at MAX_READ_BYTES.; Inline budget counts raw bytes: whole chunks within 32 KiB. If the first chunk alone is larger, its text is cut at a character boundary and ends_with_newline is false. |
| W4-R2-b | F-t3-050 R2 'brief per action in the Run Console and chat rail' (audit :1016-1026); R2 (580e32237) did the Run Console only | built | Adds the per-action brief (browser action title plus status pill) to each step button in BrowserChrome's evidence-drawer step timeline (the Tidslinje drawer), behind VITE_BROWSER_ACTION_BRIEF; with the switch off the markup is unchanged. A pure helper factsFromTimelineEntry(entry, rationale, approval) in browser-action-brief.ts builds the facts: the rationale comes from browserRationales, and the gate comes from browserApprovals, joined by action id. The chrome did not hold the step's action id, action type or observation status, so BrowserTimelineViewEntry gains an optional action field that browserSessionFromChatRun fills from the run's own events; gateway steps leave it out and read as observed. Nothing is fetched or persisted, the ZDR posture is unchanged, and nothing was compiled or run. | Gateway timeline entries carry no action id, so an approval gate cannot attach to a gateway step. That needs an action id on the Quarry timeline entry, which is a backend change.; The snapshot tabs in the browser tab bar do not show the brief (their labels are capped at 92px in compact mode). Extending them is a separate decision. Deviations: Two files outside the spec's list changed: browser-session.ts (optional action field on BrowserTimelineViewEntry) and chat-run-watch.ts (browserSessionFromChatRun forwards step.actionId, actionType, observed and status). The spec assumed the chrome already held these facts, but BrowserTimelineViewEntry carried none of them; without them the chat rail showed a generic verb and 'Done' for dispatched steps that were not yet observed.; The step's action id is the chat run's action id. Gateway steps have none, so the step number stands in as the id in factsFromTimelineEntry; the id is never shown.; The approval join matches an approval to a step only when the step carries an action id and the approval list is passed. No current wiring does both: gateway steps carry no action id and the chat rail passes no approval list. For an observed step a granted gate would not change the pill anyway.; Placement: the brief replaces the step label in the drawer's step timeline (knowledge-browser-timeline), and the pill sits in its trailing markers column. That keeps the existing three-column grid and needs no CSS change. The browser tab bar (role tablist, Nettleserfaner) and the selected-step detail panel are unchanged. The drawer is closed by default in the chat rail, so the brief appears after the user opens Tidslinje og evidens.; Observed steps with no status (all gateway steps) read 'Done' in the neutral tone, the same outcome default the Run Console uses. An unobserved chat step reads 'Sent'. |
| W4-SH-2 | F-t3-046 'an importer for ChatGPT and Claude export files' (audit :968-978); EP-12-S15 covers only verevon.thread-export.v1 | built | Added ChatGPT and Claude export readers to session-core's ImportThread. The new thread_import_foreign.rs holds pure converters: from_chatgpt walks the active branch (current_node up through parent), keeps string parts of user/assistant messages and counts other parts as dropped; from_claude reads chat_messages (human/assistant, text) and counts non-text blocks and attachments. thread_import.rs now reads any file into a shared ImportShape, so the D11 caps, role downgrade, quoting, Importert: title and digest apply to foreign files unchanged, and the verevon parse keeps its behaviour. A foreign file is read only when SESSION_THREAD_IMPORT_FOREIGN_ENABLED is on, the file has no format key, and the import switch is on; otherwise the refusal is as before. Foreign rows carry metadata.imported.source (chatgpt or claude). The ZDR/waived refusal is unchanged and runs before any parse. Nothing was compiled or run as tests; rustfmt --check passes on the new file and adds no new hunks to thread_import.rs, and the registry JSON and compose declaration were checked by script. | - Deviations: from_claude takes conversation_index as well as the raw value (the spec abbreviated it to from_claude(raw)), so both converters accept a bare object or an array the same way.; ImportError is a type alias for the import's existing Invalid enum (closed reasons shape and empty) rather than a new enum.; The ImportThread request has no index field, so the RPC reads entry 0 of a multi-conversation array and silently ignores the other conversations. The lead should decide whether to refuse arrays longer than one instead.; Foreign detection runs only when the file has no format key. A file that declares any other format is still refused as format, even with the switch on.; ImportPlan.exported_at became Option<String>. A foreign conversation with no update time gets null in metadata. Verevon rows keep their existing keys and values; only foreign rows get metadata.imported.source.; The switch reader is mp_contracts::loop_fold::switch_from, the family's strict reader, which accepts true in any letter case with surrounding whitespace rather than only lowercase true.; The registry entry is a full flags entry in name order, not an unregistered-list name, because the registry README says the unregistered list may only shrink.; For ChatGPT, MAX_IMPORT_MESSAGES counts every message on the active branch, including dropped roles, and the 64 KiB per-message cap applies to every message as the native parse does. The 2 MiB file cap covers the whole file, including branches that are not read, so a long edited conversation can be refused as too_large.; Not filtered, because the spec names no rule for them: ChatGPT messages flagged metadata.is_visually_hidden_from_conversation, and assistant messages addressed to a tool (recipient other than all) that carry plain text parts. |
| W4-SH-3 | F-t3-046 share track; ADR apps/Model Plane/docs/decisions/conversation-share-model.md decisions 3,7; D3-536 decided | built | Registered session-core as the one owner service for conversation_snapshot in owner_service.go, and changed the existing taxonomy assertion that expected no owner to expect session-core. Session-core's user-core registry row in docker-compose.yml now carries authz:read and authz:write, following the documents-api row, so its signed authz calls can pass requireVerifiedAuthzDelegation. authz_facade.go needed no change because its handlers are already type-generic. gofmt is clean on the three Go files and the edited registry parses; nothing was compiled or tested, per the rules. The view-only rule for conversation_snapshot went into applyOwnerAssertedGrantRules in authz_owner_asserted.go, next to the other owner-asserted grant rules.; The org-only requirement needed no new branch: IsActiveOrgMember already runs for every owner-asserted subject. | W4-PL-1 | F-t3-059 'Add digest, source and kill-list columns' (audit :1124-1134; plugin_packages 0003:240-259) | built | Migration 0039 adds nullable source_kind, source_ref, source_pin, manifest_digest and installed_at to plugin_packages, with a cross-column CHECK, and creates plugin_kill_list and plugin_components. plugins.go adds PluginLoaderFromLookup (strict literal "true" on PLUGIN_LOADER_ENABLED) and WithSupplyChainPins. With the switch on, create needs a full 40-hex commit or sha256 archive pin on an https URL with no credentials, query or fragment; it computes sha256 over the canonical manifest and refuses a killed digest with 422 plugin_killed inside the same INSERT. PATCH cannot change pins; pin and enable are guarded in the UPDATE's WHERE clause (stored pin, installed_at, kill list), and a refused update is classified with one follow-up read. With the switch off, the legacy SQL paths are unchanged. main.go wires the reader; the compose line and the flag-registry entry default to off. Nothing was compiled or tested; gofmt parses plugins.go cleanly. | Outside this unit's file list: model-gateway register_plugin (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/grpc.rs, around line 2214) posts without source pins, so with PLUGIN_LOADER_ENABLED=true its registrations are refused with 422 plugin_source_required until the gateway contract carries source_kind, source_ref and source_pin. Do not switch the flag on for the gateway path before that follow-up lands. Deviations: source_ref is restricted to https with no userinfo, query or fragment; the spec says only 'git URL or archive URL'. Stricter, so a credential cannot be stored or fetched in the clear.; Under the switch, PATCH refuses any source field with 409 plugin_source_immutable: a pin is set at registration and a different source is a new version. The spec's 'require on update' is read as: pinning or enabling needs the stored triple.; Unpinning a plugin also disables it, so an enabled plugin never persists unpinned. Disabling and unpinning a killed plugin still succeed.; Added DB CHECKs the spec did not list: pin shape per kind, sha256:<64 hex> on manifest_digest and plugin_kill_list.digest, and consistency across the three source columns. plugin_components.plugin_id REFERENCES plugin_packages(id) follows the mcp_oauth_tokens precedent.; Digests are stored as sha256:<64 hex>, matching the archive pin and the existing sha256: convention, not bare hex.; Typed codes beyond plugin_killed, chosen here: plugin_source_required and plugin_source_invalid (422), plugin_manifest_invalid (422), plugin_source_immutable, plugin_not_installed and plugin_changed (409). The 409 for an enable refused by state follows the registry_apis precedent (proposal_requires_decision).; main.go is edited (one line: WithSupplyChainPins(api.PluginLoaderFromLookup(os.Getenv))). It was not in the spec's file list, but without it the reader is never called.; The list and get queries always select the five new columns, since the migration runs at every boot. The JSON gains source_kind, source_ref, source_pin, manifest_digest and installed_at, omitted when NULL, so legacy rows read back unchanged.; No ZDR refusal was added: the spec has no ZDR line, and the plugin registry stores configuration (a manifest and a pin), not run content.; No admin check on pin or enable under the switch. The spec does not require one, and enabling is unreachable until the loader sets installed_at. The loader or install unit should enforce admin, as the build plan requires.; Enable and pin for a killed digest are refused with 422 plugin_killed in the UPDATE's WHERE clause; disabling and unpinning are not guarded, so a killed plugin can still be turned off.; Working-tree note: flag-registry.json and docker-compose.yml also carry other writers' hunks (INFERENCE_CATALOGUE_OVERLAY, MODEL_GATEWAY_GOAL_UNATTENDED_ENABLED, ORCHESTRATOR_GOAL_JUDGE_ACTIVITY_ENABLED, MODEL_GATEWAY_URL). My hunks are only PLUGIN_LOADER_ENABLED. cmd/main.go was already not gofmt-clean at HEAD (skillbundle import order); I left it alone.; Read-only checks run beyond gofmt -l: a gofmt -d on cmd/main.go to diagnose the pre-existing import-order hit, and git show of HEAD's main.go into the scratchpad for comparison. |
| W4-PL-2 | F-t3-059 'loader that expands a plugin into child capabilities (skills, remote HTTP MCP servers, slash commands first) ... refuse stdio, bin and script componen | built | Added the closed verevon.plugin.v1 manifest parser and the plugin loader, plus three routes behind PLUGIN_LOADER_ENABLED. GET /api/v1/plugins/{id}/components returns the per-component digests and the consent list (admin only, no bodies or URLs). POST .../install checks that the posted digests equal the installable digest set exactly, then creates every child, the plugin_components rows, the audit rows and installed_at in one transaction. POST .../uninstall removes the children, marks the components removed, disables the plugin and clears installed_at. Hooks, bin, scripts and stdio_servers are never run and come back as refused report rows. Skills go through the SkillsHandler's insert and lint rules, commands are stored as the authored kind template, and MCP servers go through normalizeMCPRegistration (https, public DNS, auth none, org scope), so their tools stay unapproved. Install is refused for a non-admin, a non-person, a ZDR or unknown-retention principal, a killed digest, a manifest that no longer matches the registered digest, an already-installed plugin, and a name conflict. Reconcile events go out after commit and registry_audit_log rows are written inside the transaction. Nothing was compiled or run (code-only rule); gofmt reports the new files clean, and cmd/main.go already had unsorted imports that gofmt flags. | - Deviations: The spec's Install(ctx, tx, plugin, consentedDigests) became a method on Loader taking a Caller: Install(ctx, tx, plugin, caller, consentedDigests). The loader does the admin, person and ZDR checks itself, and the caller is built from the signed principal, not the request body. The api package can't be imported by internal/plugins (import cycle), so the loader writes children through a ChildStore interface that api/plugin_children.go implements using SkillsHandler and MCPHandler settings and statements. plugins.go gained WithChildStores(skills, mcp). registry_apis.go was not edited.; agent_skills has no source column and migrations were out of scope, so the source tag plugin:<id>@<component digest> is recorded in three places: the skill revision log reason (set via skillActorSQL's mp.reason), the mcp_servers.description of MCP children, and plugin_components.report. Ledger: a source column on agent_skills would be a session-core migration.; plugin_components.kind allows only skill, mcp_server and command (migration 0039). Refused hooks, bin, scripts and stdio_servers rows therefore appear in the GET components report, the install response and the plugin_installed audit diff, but are not persisted in plugin_components. Persisting them would need a widened CHECK in a new migration. Skill, MCP and command components refused for lint or registration reasons are persisted with status refused.; Consent covers the installable (pending) components only; refused rows are listed for information and are not part of the consent set. An install that declares no installable component is refused as plugin_manifest_invalid.; Children are created enabled, with org scope. MCP servers use rollout canary and risk high and keep their tools unapproved. A name already taken in the org fails the whole install with 409 plugin_component_conflict, and the transaction rolls back; the loader never overwrites an existing child.; Uninstall deletes skills and templates the way the skill DELETE route does (bundle cleanup under retention) and soft-deletes MCP servers with the tool cascade. This frees the names for a reinstall. It also sets plugin enabled=false and installed_at=NULL. There is no kill-list API route, so the kill path is Uninstall with reason killed, ready for a later caller.; Install and components first recompute the canonical digest of the stored manifest_json and compare it with manifest_digest. A mismatch answers 409 plugin_changed (a jsonb number normalisation could also trigger it). The manifest name and version must match the registered row.; Install and uninstall are POSTs, which authz.AuthorizeHTTP already refuses for ZDR or unknown-retention principals before the handler. The loader repeats the install ZDR refusal as defense in depth. |
| W4-PL-3 | F-t3-059 'starts with an org-curated catalogue', kill list | built | Built the org-curated plugin catalogue and kill list, dark behind PLUGIN_LOADER_ENABLED. capability-core gets GET /api/v1/plugin-catalog, approve and unapprove (org admin, own rows only), and POST/DELETE /api/v1/plugin-kill-list (org scope for org admins, global scope for a platform service principal holding capability:global:write) in a new internal/api/plugin_catalog.go, wired in cmd/main.go. A kill writes the entry, clears catalogue approval and uninstalls every installed copy of the digest through a new Loader.UninstallKilled, all in one transaction. The BFF pass-through is in agent_actions.rs (catalogue, approve/unapprove, components, install, uninstall, kill list; session required, no BFF admin gate). The BFF reaches capability-core only through model-gateway, so I added the matching proxies in model-gateway http_routes.rs, plus six surface-contract rows that its non-GET lint requires. catalog_approved went into the unmerged W4-PL-1 migration 0039. Nothing was compiled or run; gofmt is clean on plugin_catalog.go and loader.go, rustfmt shows no new hunks in the Rust code I added, and main.go's import order was already unsorted at HEAD. | - Deviations: Route chain: the BFF reaches capability-core only through model-gateway (BFF /api/v1/* -> model-gateway /v1/* -> capability-core /api/v1/*), and model-gateway had no proxies for these routes. I added seven proxies in model-gateway http_routes.rs (approve, unapprove, kill POST, kill DELETE, components, install, uninstall). They call the existing proxy_to_capability_core and validate the plugin id segment and the digest/scope values. That file was not in the spec's file list.; Surface contract: model-gateway's every_non_get_route_is_classified test needs a space-surface row per non-GET route. I added six rows (zdr_class refuse, basis guard:proxy_to_capability_core) to api/contracts/space-surface.v1.json and the identical vendored copy in rust/services/model-gateway/tests/fixtures/. The two .sha256 pins were NOT touched. They were already stale at HEAD (pin da4ab5f7..., golden 51859005... at HEAD). Lead action: re-pin both files to 5ed1256250c9fbb8312fb2e1c7b3f8c2e3e9c1d544fa02311585594c522c24cc and run zdr-contract-sync.; The kill path calls a new sibling Loader.UninstallKilled(ctx, tx, plugin, actorID) instead of Uninstall. Uninstall requires an org admin acting as a person, which a platform service principal can never be, so a global kill could not go through it. The removal body moved unchanged into an unexported uninstall() that both entry points call. Uninstall's behaviour is unchanged. The spec said 'calls loader Uninstall'.; catalog_approved is declared BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE (spec: BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE). It is a tightening with no effect on valid writes.; Platform principal: a global kill needs PrincipalType 'service', OrganizationID 'global' and the existing authz.GlobalWriteScope (capability:global:write, the scope reserved for process-wide registries). I added no new scope constant and no Control Plane config. The HTTP write rule still needs capability:write and a non-ZDR posture.; Approve requires an org-owned package that is pinned, has its source and digest, and is not killed. These are checked in the UPDATE's WHERE clause, with typed refusals otherwise. Unapprove is always allowed. A kill clears catalog_approved on every row with that digest in its scope, including rows that are not installed.; Global-registered plugins (org_id 'global') are not listed in an org's catalogue, and an org admin cannot approve them. catalog_approved is a row column, so approving a global row would change it for every org.; DELETE /api/v1/plugin-kill-list takes digest and scope as query parameters, because the model-gateway proxy helper cannot forward a DELETE body. POST takes {digest, scope, reason} as JSON. The org is never taken from the body or the query. scope defaults to org.; The BFF adds no admin gate on the new routes, following 'admin checks stay server-side'. capability-core enforces the org admin, ZDR and platform rules. The existing plugin create/update/delete BFF routes keep their can_author_skills gate unchanged.; Every catalogue and kill-list write refuses a ZDR or unstated retention posture (the same predicate as the loader and the org hook writers). Reads are open to any org member, ZDR included.; Observation, not changed (plugin_children.go belongs to W4-PL-2): RemoveSkill writes actor kind 'user' for every actor, so a platform kill's skill revisions name a service id under kind user. Also, DELETE /api/v1/plugins/{id} soft-deletes without uninstalling, so an installed plugin's children stay live until a kill or an uninstall. A kill does reach soft-deleted rows. |

The new PluginInstallDialog.tsx reads /components, lists every component with kind, name, digest and refusal reason, requires one consent tick per installable kind, sends exactly the server's consent_digests, and shows the server's component report after install. PluginsSection.tsx, flag on, shows source pin, manifest digest, installed/pinned/catalogue/kill chips, and admin controls (pin, install, uninstall, list/unlist, kill with reason). Non-admins get the read-only catalogue. Flag off, the section renders as before. The other changed files are Control Plane Go and JSON and Ingestion Plane Go, so nothing is left 'not type-checked' there.

Ledger notes (to be moved into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **W3-EP-7-S6-b**: EP-7 S6, orchestrator side: JudgeGoalTurnActivity is built and registered, and dark behind ORCHESTRATOR_GOAL_JUDGE_ACTIVITY_ENABLED (default off). The activity carries ids only in Temporal history. It presents orchestrator-core's model-gateway token (model:goal_judge, org-pinned, persistent retention in the registry) and its inference-core bearer. Live judging stays blocked until the owner decides on a persistent judge principal for model-gateway's session-core credential; until then the gateway answers 503 goal_judge_unavailable and no verdict is recorded. Session-core's RecordGoalVerdict is idempotent per turn, so Temporal retries cannot double-record a verdict.
- **W4-BYO-4**: W4-BYO-4 built (code only, uncompiled, no tests written). The customer-gateway connection and inference broker live in integration-corev2: the internal/customergateway package, internal/api/customer_gateway.go, and wiring in server.go, config.go and cmd/api/main.go. The provider key and connector type are `openai_compatible_gateway`. The gateway API key is sealed with the service vault (AAD = connection id) in the connection's encrypted access-token column and is never returned, logged or leased: the oauth service now returns ErrBrokerOnlyCredential for this provider, and a generic disconnect skips provider revocation. The ZDR assessment is always not_supported (basis not_assessed), written in the same audit transaction as the connection and built outside the pinned vendored contract with the shared zdr.Digest. The provider is intentionally absent from the provider catalog so the ZDR reconcile does not overwrite it, and this connection cannot use the Z-13 acknowledgement API. The Model Plane routes are /internal/model-connections/customer-gateway/infer and /infer/stream, authenticated with the Model Plane internal key. Audit rows carry ids, model and outcome only. The whole feature is dark behind CUSTOMER_GATEWAY_CONNECTIONS_ENABLED. Open for the lead: compile and fmt check; add the env var to the integration-api compose block; Model Plane's client side (calling the new routes and ZDR-floor handling) is a separate unit.
- **W4-BYO-5**: W4-BYO-5 (inference-core, code only, not compiled): a call that names model_connection_id is served only by the customer-gateway adapter, which calls Integration Core's /internal/model-connections/customer-gateway/infer and /infer/stream with the service key and the opaque connection id. No platform provider, cache, intent or role layer sees such a call. With INFERENCE_BYO_GATEWAY_ENABLED off, such a call is refused as unimplemented. ZDR (by token or request flag), waived and api-key principals, a privacy or residency floor above Global, images, tools and structured output are refused before any send. The served-by id is customer-gateway.<connection id>, and usage is logged with billing=customer_connection. Model-gateway's cost basis must classify the customer-gateway. prefix as customer-billed before the switch is enabled.
- **W4-BYO-6**: W4-BYO-6 (model-gateway, BFF, SPA), dark: a customer-gateway connection is named on a chat turn only while MODEL_GATEWAY_BYO_GATEWAY is the literal true. The turn is text-only (no tools, search, images, deep research, plan, agent runs, attachments or structured output) and never zero retention: normalize and the posture resolver refuse a ZDR or temporary turn on a connection route. Its auxiliary calls go to the same gateway, no compaction summary is made, and the gateway id is carried through send, retry, regenerate and edit in the SPA. Not yet safe to enable: a reloaded turn's regenerate, edit or retry is not connection-bound, because session-core does not persist the connection id. Integration Core's list and delete routes are also still missing.
- **W4-BYO-1**: W4-BYO-1 (F-t3-065 phase 1): the platform catalogue overlay is an optional catalogue_overlay on the routing policy, holding hidden model ids, a listing order and model-id aliases. Every policy write validates it against the chat catalogue (400 invalid_catalogue_overlay). Behind INFERENCE_CATALOGUE_OVERLAY=true (default off), ListModels drops hidden models and lists each alias as its target, and admission maps an alias to its target and refuses a pinned hidden id with typed kind model_hidden (NOT_FOUND). Off, a stored overlay is ignored. The overlay has no privacy tier, residency or ZDR field, and the entitlement check still runs after it. Downstream refusal catalogues will need the model_hidden kind.
- **W4-BYO-2**: W4-BYO-2 (frontend): the router-policy page edits the platform catalogue overlay (hide, order, aliases) behind VITE_CATALOGUE_OVERLAY, default off. The page checks the overlay with W4-BYO-1's rules and saves the whole policy document as before. The save carries expected_version when the loaded document has a version and reloads the policy on 409. The server does not return a version or honour expected_version yet, so that path is dormant. Built against the working-tree (uncommitted) W4-BYO-1 in inference-core: catalogue_overlay.rs, routing_policy.rs, grpc.rs and http_health.rs.
- **W4-BYO-3**: W4-BYO-3 (EP-13 D6/DEC-13, F-t3-065): per-organisation routing overlay, narrow-only, dark behind SESSION_ORG_ROUTING_OVERLAY (session-core) and INFERENCE_ORG_OVERLAY (inference-core). session-core stores one JSONB row per org in org_routing_overlays (migration 0117, erased with the org). The document is `{hidden[], order[], roles{purpose:[<=4 ids]}}` and nothing else: no aliases, no way to add a model. The RoutingPolicy service gained GetOrgRoutingOverlay and SetOrgRoutingOverlay, which answer UNAVAILABLE when the switch is off. A write needs the caller's own org, an admin-scoped user or a service with scope `session:org-routing:write` (the gateway checks the org-admin role), and refuses ZDR and waived callers. inference-core reads the overlay through the policy client, cached 120 s and 1000 orgs, fail-soft to the last value or empty. It applies the overlay to the model listing after the platform overlay. Role resolution tries the org's candidates first via role::choose_with_org, then the platform table. Org candidates must be catalogue-listed and not reserved, not hidden by the org or the platform overlay, and for small-budget purposes must accept a no-reasoning call. Each is still gated by entitlement, ZDR, privacy-tier and residency in role_candidate_admitted. Route note role_source is `org_table` when an org candidate wins. Known limits: an org-hidden pinned model is not refused at admission (listing and roles only); the write scope grant and the gateway route are not built; Go, Python and TS proto bindings are not regenerated; nothing is compiled or live-proven."]
- **W4-IMG-1**: W4-IMG-1 (2026-11-10): inference-core GenerateImageRequest.strict_provider = 9 (proto, additive, default false). With the flag on, VisionChain::generate_image serves from the one provider that matches the hint and lists the requested image model, returns that provider's error as it is, and never walks the chain. No match is refused as image_model_unavailable (ProviderError::ImageModelUnavailable, NOT_FOUND, kind trailer) before any provider call. Served-by fields are always filled. No switch and no compose or flag-registry change. Open for the lead: regenerate Go and Python bindings and the Verevon TS gen copy of inference.proto; regenerate apps/Model Plane/api/contracts/infer-statuses.json (MP_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 on inference-core), since the new kind image_model_unavailable and the case provider_image_model_unavailable change it, and update the digest pin in scripts/tests/zdr-contract-sync.test.sh; sync the Data Plane fixture copies if that test requires equality; W4-IMG-3 must pass strict_provider=true and surface image_model_unavailable.
- **W4-M2**: 2026-11-10 - MCP app frame takes the server's network policy, client-validated (W4-M2). Status: built dark behind VITE_MCP_APPS_NETWORK (frontend, default off, literal 'true') and the existing MCP_APPS_NETWORK_ENABLED (model-gateway, default off); not compiled or run, not deployed. The frame draws the app as a fetched srcdoc document, which answers the M1c open point: no iframe src on the BFF origin. McpAppResource reads csp and network_origins; validatedAppCsp accepts the server policy only when it names PREVIEW_CSP's directives in order and differs only by canonical https origins in connect-src (in place of 'none'), img-src, font-src and media-src. Anything else gives PREVIEW_CSP and the 'needs network' notice; a validated policy that grants an origin hides the notice. The BFF needed no change (proxy_model_json_with_delegations relays the upstream JSON as Value). The sandbox is unchanged (allow-scripts). D12 holds unless both switches are on and the policy validates. Open: model-gateway's mcp_app_csp::build writes resourceDomains origins into script-src and style-src (baseUri and frame origins into base-uri and frame-src), which this allowlist refuses; every resource origin brings script-src with it, so today only connect-src-only grants can pass. The two sides need reconciling before an app that loads CDN scripts works. Owner: document VITE_MCP_APPS_NETWORK in the verevonv3 .env.example (not in this unit's file list).
- **W4-P3**: W4-P3 (F-t3-035): the owner's background-process output read is reachable from the SPA through the gateway at GET /api/v1/chat/runs/{run_id}/processes/{pid}/output, behind CHAT_PROCESS_OUTPUT_READ_ENABLED (default off). The route mints the caller's sandbox-manager bearer and a server-side Control read decision for model-plane-sandbox-manager, then relays Model Gateway's P2 route unchanged, so Model Gateway's ZDR run_inspection refusal reaches the browser as sent. Built on branch mp-w345 in code only: not compiled, not tested, not live-proven.
- **W4-P4**: W4-P4 (EP-1, F-t3-035): ChatLiveRunPanel gains a computer panel tab strip (Browser, Terminal, Files) behind VITE_CHAT_COMPUTER_PANEL, default off, and never mounted on a zero-retention turn. The Terminal tab follows a background process's output through GET /api/v1/chat/runs/{run}/processes/{pid}/output (W4-P3), polling every two seconds while the process is alive, and shows the output as plain text only. A refused read (404 or 403) shows the transcript with a note. The Files tab draws the latest workspace file call from the transcript. No new action contract: chat.open_computer_viewer already exists and is unchanged. Built in code only; not compiled, linted or tested.
- **W4-SH-1**: EP-12 S15 BFF and SPA entry built dark. The gateway relays POST /api/v1/chat/threads/import to Model Gateway under the owner's model and session bearers, with a 2 MiB body cap and the file as the raw body and the title on the query string, and returns Model Gateway's answer and typed refusals unchanged. The SPA has the thread.import action contract (registry entry; model access exempt as no_owner_executor, context_write), importThread() in chat-client.ts, and ImportConversationAction behind VITE_THREAD_IMPORT (default off), mounted beside export and fork on the latest answer. The server switches SESSION_THREAD_IMPORT_ENABLED and MODEL_GATEWAY_THREAD_EXPORT stay off. The admission golden must be regenerated at integration because a new ActionId and model-access row were added. Not verified in a browser or by tests: whether the sidebar lists the new thread before its next refresh; a 2 MiB import can exceed the gateway's shared 25 s client timeout, and a timed-out import may still be created upstream, so a retry could make a second thread; the title appears in the query string of the internal Model Gateway call.
- **W4-P6**: W4-P6 (2026-11-10): the process_read spill is dark behind EXECUTION_PROCESS_OUTPUT_SPILL (default off). A spill is refused on ZDR and waived runs under the feature process_output_spill, writes only to <workspace>/.tmp/process-output, and is left out of the lease-release upload, so it is never durable. Open decision: workspace read and grep refuse files over 1 MiB, so a spill above that size is readable only from code_interpreter or by paging with process_read. Decide whether to raise that limit or cap the spill at 1 MiB.
- **W4-R2-b**: W4-R2-b (F-t3-050 R2): the per-action brief also renders on BrowserChrome's evidence-drawer step timeline, behind VITE_BROWSER_ACTION_BRIEF (off). The chat-run adapter now forwards each step's action id, action type, observed flag and observation status on BrowserTimelineViewEntry.action; gateway steps carry none. Approval gates join by action id and reach no timeline step in the current wiring. No fetch and no persistence; ZDR posture unchanged.
- **W4-SH-2**: W4-SH-2 (EP-12 S15 foreign readers): ImportThread also reads a ChatGPT conversations.json or a Claude data-export conversations.json while SESSION_THREAD_IMPORT_FOREIGN_ENABLED is the literal true (default off; it also needs SESSION_THREAD_IMPORT_ENABLED on). ChatGPT is read along the active branch (current_node up through parent); Claude is read from chat_messages. Only user and assistant text is kept. Every turn goes through the same caps, untrusted user-role quoting and title as a verevon import, and gets metadata.imported.source set to chatgpt or claude. ZDR and waived callers are refused before any parse. No proto change. Open: the RPC reads entry 0 of a multi-conversation array because the request has no index field.
- **W4-SH-3**: W4-SH-3 (Control): session-core is the one owner service for conversation_snapshot (authztaxonomy ownerServices). Its user-core row (USER_CORE_SESSION_TOKEN, presented as USER_CORE_SERVICE_TOKEN) now carries authz:read and authz:write, as the documents-api row does. Owner-asserted grants for conversation_snapshot are org-only and view-only (applyOwnerAssertedGrantRules). Writes and lists stay behind CONTROL_OWNER_ASSERTED_GRANTS (default false). /authz/check and /authz/visible are not flag-gated, so session-core can reach them with a verified v2 delegation, and they answer only for the delegated user. Dependency for W4-SH-4: send X-Service-Id session-core and X-Service-Token (USER_CORE_SERVICE_TOKEN), v2 delegation headers HMAC-signed with that token, and the owner's bearer proof. No migration, no proto change, no new switch, no Space grant, no edit role.
- **W4-PL-1**: F-t3-059 supply-chain pins (W4-PL-1), dark behind PLUGIN_LOADER_ENABLED (default off, literal true). With the switch on, plugin registration needs a full commit SHA (git_commit) or a sha256 archive pin on an https URL with no credentials, query or fragment. The API computes manifest_digest as sha256 over the canonical JSON of manifest_json (object keys sorted). A killed digest (plugin_kill_list, global or per org) is refused at create, pin and enable with 422 plugin_killed. Enabling needs a pinned plugin with installed_at set, which only the loader writes. Source pins are immutable through PATCH, and unpinning disables. Kill-list entries are written by SQL only; an entry added after enablement does not disable the plugin, so the loader must re-check the list when it loads. Migration 0039.
- **W4-PL-2**: W4-PL-2 built: internal/plugins/manifest.go (closed verevon.plugin.v1 parser; skill and command bodies at most 64 KiB; MCP allowlist at most 64; https URLs only; unknown keys refused; hooks, bin, scripts and stdio_servers recognised only to be refused) and loader.go (Plan, Install, Uninstall). Routes GET /api/v1/plugins/{id}/components, POST .../install and POST .../uninstall are mounted only when PLUGIN_LOADER_ENABLED is the literal true and the child stores are wired. Authority: org admin acting as a person, non-ZDR, with consent_digests exactly equal to the installable component digests. Children are written through the SkillsHandler store and lint rules (commands as authored template, needing AUTHORED_KINDS_ENABLED) and through normalizeMCPRegistration (auth none, org scope, tools unapproved). The whole install is one transaction with plugin_components, registry_audit_log rows and installed_at, followed by reconcile events. Open points for the ledger: (1) the source tag lives in the revision reason, the MCP description and plugin_components.report, because agent_skills has no source column; (2) refused hook, bin, script and stdio rows are not persisted because plugin_components.kind is limited to three kinds, so persisting them needs a widened CHECK; (3) there is no kill-list write route, and the kill path is Uninstall with reason killed.
- **W4-PL-3**: F-t3-059 W4-PL-3, built and dark behind PLUGIN_LOADER_ENABLED. capability-core owns an org-curated plugin catalogue and a kill list. An org admin lists a pinned, unkilled package of the org in the catalogue; any org member reads the list, ZDR included. A kill writes the entry and, in one transaction, clears catalogue approval and uninstalls every installed copy of the digest through the loader. An org admin kills for the org; a platform service principal holding capability:global:write kills for every org. ZDR and unstated-posture principals cannot write either. Global-registered plugins are not catalogue-approvable by an org.
- **W4-PL-4**: W4-PL-4 (SPA, mp-w345): plugin catalogue and install-consent dialog behind VITE_PLUGIN_LOADER (default off). Five plugins.* action contracts with model access none, so the agent is never offered them. The install dialog consents to the exact component digests and shows the server report. Kill state is per-view only because the kill list has no read route in W4-PL-3. Outstanding: admission golden regeneration, gateway dispatcher arms for plugins.*, a lift-kill contract, and the measured registry count in action-registry.test.ts.

It holds the strip's layout, on the same tokens as ChangedFilesCard.css.; The mount sits after the verevon-chat-message__content block, outside it, so the PDF clone of the answer never carries the strip. The spec's ~:897 has drifted: the mount is now about line 987 of AssistantMessage, before the EP-2 research-plan block.; 'Safe-link confirmation' is read as the isSafeLinkHref gate, with target=_blank and rel='noopener noreferrer' as the citations use. No click-through prompt was added. A hit whose page link or thumbnail fails the check, or whose thumbnail is not http(s), is dropped rather than shown unlinked.; Settled means not streaming, not waiting and not error, the same gate as the action row. A stopped turn counts as settled.; The failure test is narrationState(call) === 'done', the activity line's own test. A tool call with no status counts as unfinished, so if the server drops tool-call status on save, reloaded answers show no strip. This fails closed, and it needs a live check on reload.; When a turn ran several web searches, the query of the first finished one is used.; A failed image request settles as no images for the session and is not retried, which follows from the once-per-turn rule.; The session cache is module memory. It is not cleared on sign-out and lasts until the page reloads. |

| W5-069-a | F-t3-069 (plan 3.3.4; audit card F-t3-069 side chat); ledger.md 2026-11-07 entry says side chat/spin-off not built | built | Built W5-069-a: POST /v1/threads/:thread_id/side-chat in the new side_chat_routes.rs, dark behind MODEL_GATEWAY_SIDE_CHAT_ENABLED (404 side_chat_disabled unless the literal true). The handler checks a 1 to 2000 character question (held as UserContent), takes the zdr posture from zdr_posture::zdr_or_refuse, refuses a Space thread with 409 scope_not_available_in_space, reads the newest 40 active-path turns with list_conversation (human posts excluded), keeps the newest 24,000 characters as a fenced transcript, and makes one tool-less call at temperature 0.2 with purpose ANSWER. It returns data.answer, model_used and usage, and writes nothing. The gateway has no read-only signal for it: ListConversation, ListThreads and ListRuns (RunDetail) carry no retention class. Closing it needs a session-core flag or read, which is outside this unit's file list. A thread the caller does not own is not listed, and ListConversation refuses it as before.; InferPurpose is ANSWER (the user-visible answer to a turn). The review route uses VALIDATION; the spec names no purpose. Request id is '<fresh ULID>-side-chat'.; Typed errors beyond the spec: 400 side_chat_question_missing and 400 side_chat_question_too_long (refused, not cut, as review does); 400 invalid_thread_request for an empty thread id; 502 side_chat_no_answer when the model returns no text.; The fence id is a fresh ULID because no run exists, so the spec's run-id markers are not available. max_tokens is 1500 (the spec gives none); temperature is 0.2 as specified.; Snapshot cut rule: the newest characters up to exactly 24,000. The oldest kept turn is cut from its start to fill the budget. A line saying earlier text is not included is added when older turns exist or a cut happened. All roles (user, assistant, system, tool) are included, labelled by role.; Credential: the route takes VerifiedSessionBearer, the session token that http_routes aliases as VerifiedModelBearer. The VerifiedModelBearer in auth.rs is a token-less marker and is not used.; The question is held as UserContent, as the W5-075-b note asks of the side-chat question. |

The spec's 401/403 to 503 mapping therefore reaches the SPA as delegated_auth_unavailable, not the typed ZDR code. | W5-074-a | F-t3-074 (audit card: start with a server-derived target and an admin-authored, audited org-scope rule table; EvaluatePolicyRequest carries no target) | built | Added target_kind (field 8) and target_value (field 9) to EvaluatePolicyRequest as additive, documented fields; the gap existed (fields 1-7 only, no derivation, no switch). In execution-core, capability_policy.rs gained pub fn derive_target, which reads the target from the tool input: the host of a web_fetch or browser.open URL, the first recipient's domain of a provider mail send (is_mail_send), or the first command token of a shell call or an sh code_interpreter program. The runtime loop now calls a new trait method evaluate_tool_call_with_input carrying the input and the zdr-or-waived flag; GrpcCapabilityPolicy names the target on the request only while CAPABILITY_PERMISSION_RULES_ENABLED is the literal true and sends the kind alone on retention-bound runs. The switch is declared in the Model Plane compose under execution-core (default false) and registered in the flag registry with the one matching declaration. Nothing was compiled or run; rustfmt --check shows no diff in the new code (the file's pre-existing diffs are unchanged), and the registry JSON parses and stays name-sorted. | - Deviations: Trait seam: evaluate_tool_call takes no tool input, so the input could not reach the policy. Added a default trait method evaluate_tool_call_with_input (the runtime loop's single production call site uses it). Its default delegates to evaluate_tool_call, so every test double and other CapabilityPolicy implementor compiles unchanged. evaluate_tool_call itself passes an empty input, which names no target.; ZDR: the value is withheld on zdr OR waived runs (the retention-bound condition execution-core already uses for content gates), not on zdr alone. Consequence: a rule that matches a target value does not apply on a waived run either. Owner to confirm this reading.; shell: its input is {program, args} with no command field, so the target is the program token; args are not read (so sh -c '...' names sh).; code_interpreter: only language sh names a target (its first token). A python program names none, because the first word of Python source can be a string literal that carries content.; Command token: leading NAME=value assignments are skipped so an assigned value never becomes the target; the token is cut at the first character outside ASCII letters, digits and ._-/+ and capped at 64 characters. A quoted or escaped first word yields no target.; registrable host is read as the URL host (lowercase, no port, trailing dot removed). The Rust workspace has no public-suffix list and no new dependency can be added without cargo, so no eTLD+1 derivation.; Mail: the send set is provider_writes::is_mail_send (the module has no separate send-set constant). Only the first recipient of body.to is named; cc, bcc and later recipients are not read, so a send that spans several domains is matched on its first domain only. Messaging sends (Slack, WhatsApp, Messenger) carry no recipient domain and get no target.; model-gateway's EvaluatePolicyRequest literal (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/grpc.rs, check_permission) uses ..Default::default() and needed no change; the Go and Python bindings were not regenerated (lead). |

Each child has a Stop button, and Stop all cancels the parent run; both go through the existing cancelRun. Reads repeat every 3 s while the parent or a child is still unfinished and stop on unmount. A failed read or stop shows typed copy (403 and 404 included), and no row is invented. | - Deviations: Polling also continues while the parent run is unfinished, not only while a child is, so children that attach later appear; it also continues while the lineage read is failing for now. MODEL_GATEWAY_REVIEW_RUN_ENABLED reads the literal true case-insensitively (switch_from), and its purpose says so. |

The spec lists only org_flag_values, but 'audited like web policy writes' needs a durable outbox row written in the same transaction, following the 027 pattern.; service_auth.go was edited, though it is not in the spec's file list. Without scope entries, serviceScopesForRequest returns nil and the middleware answers 403 to every call on the new routes. Added: GET /orgs/:id/flags maps to org:read:self; PUT /orgs/:id/flags/:name maps to org:settings:write:self; GET /internal/orgs/:orgId/flags maps to a new allowlisted scope org:flags:read:any, which no credential holds in code.; 'For the BFF' cannot be the gateway principal. validateServiceCredential refuses every :any scope for verevon-gateway, so that principal cannot use the internal route. The registry's compose scanner covers only the Model Plane compose, and ORG_RETENTION_POLICY_ENABLED, the closest analogue, has no compose line either. The code default is off, and only the literal "true" enables the routes.; Writes accept a JSON boolean or the strings "true"/"false" for a switch and store lower-case text. An unknown name returns 422 unknown_flag and an invalid value returns 422 invalid_value, matching the web policy's validation codes rather than 404.; PUT takes no If-Match, because the spec gives none. Last writer wins, and the per-row revision moves on by one per write. Member reads hide updated_by and updated_at from non-admins, as the web policy hides its writer.; Audit drain gap, the same as the existing web policy outbox. | W5-079-c | F-t3-079 served in the BFF boot payload, cached per service with TTL | partial | Built the BFF read and the SPA reader for organisation flags. GET /api/v1/org-flags (session required) reads org-core's internal flags route with the gateway's service credential, reuses one organisation's answer for 30 seconds through the gateway cache, and answers no-store; any failure answers {data:{flags:{},stale:true}}, and org-core's 404 passes through. The SPA's loadOrgFlags() makes the session's one read, and orgFlag(name, fallback) reads it, with a failed read reading off. The reader's attributes and dyslexia font now honour the organisation's value, and readerPolishOn() is the build switch AND that value. Code only: nothing was compiled, tested or run beyond one rustfmt --check on the new Rust file. | ChatPage.tsx (the chapter outline: a memo and a Show, two sites) and ChatMessages.tsx (data-turn-id, two sites) still read the build-only readerPolishEnabled, so the outline and turn ids ignore the organisation's value until they switch to readerPolishOn(). Both files are outside this unit's file list and are modified by other writers. Deviations: Flag name: the spec's orgFlag('chat.reader_polish', true) uses 'VITE_CHAT_READER_POLISH', the name org-core stores values under (OrgOverridableFlags in org_flags.go and flag-registry.json). A dotted name would never match a stored value.; readerPolishEnabled stays the build switch alone. The organisation's value arrives in an async read after import, so folding it into that module constant would silently do nothing. The AND is exported as readerPolishOn(). The reader's attributes and dyslexia font apply the organisation's value reactively through a new optional orgAllows dep on ReaderAppearanceDeps; the existing reader-polish tests do not pass it, so their behaviour is unchanged.; Failure semantics: a failed read (5xx, 401, 403 such as a missing org-core grant, a timeout over 5 seconds, or a malformed body) reads every presentation flag as off, overriding the call-site default. This follows the spec's 'falls back to its default (off)'. Before the read lands, and when org-core's 404 (switch off) or a 403 organization_membership_required (no organisation) comes back, the call-site default applies, so the default deployment behaves as it did before.; Cache: the 30-second per-organisation cache is the gateway's existing AppState cache (state.cache). It is Redis-backed when GATEWAY_CACHE_REDIS_URL is set and does no caching otherwise. Entries are keyed with scoped_key('org-flags', org, ['flags']) and written with store_for_secs/lookup_within. A new AppState field would have meant editing six AppState literals, so none were added or touched.; revision is the sum of org-core's per-flag stored revisions, because the internal answer carries no organisation-level revision. The sum moves on every write.; domains/mod.rs gets the module declaration as specified. main.rs also gets one .merge(domains::org_flags::router(...)) line, which the route needs to be served; the spec's file list did not include main.rs. |

Compile fix, Model Plane Rust workspace and protos: green. 0 fixes.

Compile fix, Go modules, JSON/YAML, SQL migrations, proto bindings (everything except the Mod: green. 6 fixes.

Compile fix, Frontend Plane verevonv3 (SolidJS SPA and apps/gateway BFF): green. 4 fixes. Unresolved: No TypeScript packages outside verevonv3 were changed (other changes in the tree are Go, proto and Rust), so nothing was left unchecked there.

Ledger notes (to be moved into the decision ledger when a unit is proven):

- **W4-IMG-3**: W4-IMG-3 (wave 4, F-t3-051 step 1): generate_image is a typed chat tool, built dark behind MODEL_GATEWAY_IMAGE_TOOL (default off). It is offered only on a persistent, non-conversation-only, non-customer-gateway, non-subscription turn whose org capability policy for cap.inference.image does not withhold it. A zero-retention or waived turn is refused with zdr_feature_not_supported (feature image_generation) before any provider call. A run gets at most four images, one at a time. Each provider call is strict-provider (W4-IMG-1) with no fallback, and each served call publishes an image_generation usage event for cost-core (W4-IMG-2). The image reaches the person as an image artifact; the model sees only artifact_id, model_used, provider_used, revised_prompt and size. Billed calls are never retried and are not cut at a validation deadline. Not compiled or tested in this batch. The ZDR feature row is in apps/Model Plane/docs/ZDR.md.
- **W4-IO-1**: W4-IO-1 (F-t3-044 image strip) is built dark behind VITE_CHAT_IMAGE_STRIP (frontend, default off, strict 'true'). A settled answer whose web search finished shows up to six image results, each a thumbnail linked to its page through isSafeLinkHref, with the source host and title. The images come from the existing loadSearchImages, asked once per turn when the strip scrolls into view and held in memory for the session only. The strip never mounts in a temporary chat (zero retention), a Space thread, or while the answer streams or waits. It sits outside the answer's content, so the PDF export does not carry it. Only web_search and web.search qualify. Open for a live check: confirm that a reloaded answer's stored tool calls keep their status, because the failure test reads it. Not compiled or tested.
- **W4-R3-a**: W4-R3-a (F-t3-050), browser walkthrough in RunProofBundle, dark behind SESSION_PROOF_WALKTHROUGH_ENABLED (default false, literal true). Proto: BrowserWalkthrough, BrowserWalkthroughStep, RunProofBundle.browser_walkthrough = 8, references only, never bytes. Store: list_browser_walkthrough_for_run reads BROWSER_ACTION_DISPATCHED and BROWSER_OBSERVATION_RECEIVED from events, tenant-scoped and ordered by ts. The read is bounded at two rows per step plus one, joined by (plan_id, action_id), capped at 200 steps with truncated set. The builder and handler omit the section and name "walkthrough" in unavailable when the switch is off, when the run's receipt is zdr or zdr_waived, or when the receipt cannot be read. OPEN ZDR ISSUE, not changed here: persist_browser_event (orchestration_grpc.rs, BROWSER_ACTION_DISPATCHED arm) stores the model's reason text and the action url for every run, and the execution-core publishers (browser_events.rs action_dispatched, observation_received, publish) have no zdr check, so zdr browser runs write those rows today. The retained section's text ("its content was not persisted") is therefore not true for such runs. Owner decision needed: gate persist_browser_event on retention class, and strip reason and url at the emitter. Consumers of the unavailable list should accept the new "walkthrough" id. Go and Python proto bindings still need regeneration by the lead.
- **W4-R3-b**: W4-R3-b (frontend): the Run Console's proof bundle shows the run's browser walkthrough as a keyboard-scrollable filmstrip of the screenshots the run already recorded, behind VITE_RUN_PROOF_WALKTHROUGH (default off, literal "true" only). It reads the W4-R3-a browser_walkthrough section, which the backend omits for ZDR or waived runs, so no frame renders for them. The strip stores nothing and adds no fetch beyond the screenshot reads. Live proof must still confirm that the browser artifact route serves Run Console runs that are not chat runs.
- **W5-069-a**: 2026-11-10 - Side chat is built dark (F-t3-069, W5-069-a). POST /v1/threads/:id/side-chat sits behind MODEL_GATEWAY_SIDE_CHAT_ENABLED (off). It reads the newest 40 active-path turns through Session Core, without human posts, and keeps the newest 24,000 characters as a fenced transcript. One call with no tools, at temperature 0.2 and purpose ANSWER, answers the question. The caller's zdr posture is forwarded to inference-core, and cost is null on ZDR. A Space thread is refused with scope_not_available_in_space (DEC-06), found through the owner-bound thread listing because the conversation read carries no space id. Nothing is written, and a waived token is a route mismatch. Status: implemented in source, switch off, not compiled and not live-verified. Not built: the SPA side-chat surface (needs EP-3), usage attribution for side-chat calls (decide before the switch is turned on), and the refusal of threads that hold a zdr_waived run (see not_done). This closes the gateway half of side chat named in the 2026-11-07 rewind entry.
- **W5-069-b**: W5-069-b built on mp-w345 (code only, not compiled or tested). The BFF route POST /api/v1/chat/threads/{thread_id}/side-chat (json_handlers::ask_side_chat, registered in chat.rs) forwards {question, zdr} to Model Gateway with the caller's model, session and inference credentials; upstream 401/403 are answered 503 delegated_auth_unavailable and the response is marked Cache-Control no-store. The chat action contract gains thread.side_chat (read) and thread.spin_off (no server change; the browser holds the draft until the person sends it). chat-client.ts gains askSideChat and SideChatAnswer, resolving null on 404 side_chat_disabled. No switch of its own: dark behind the upstream MODEL_GATEWAY_SIDE_CHAT_ENABLED, with VITE_CHAT_SIDE_CHAT in W5-069-c. Open items for the lead: the 403 remap hides the typed zdr_posture_mismatch code (nothing processed or stored); the BFF has no per-thread ZDR input, so the UI guard in W5-069-c is the control for ZDR and temporary threads.
- **W5-069-c**: W5-069-c (Model Plane wave 5, Frontend Plane): the side chat panel is built dark behind VITE_CHAT_SIDE_CHAT (literal 'true', default off, typed in vite-env.d.ts). Answers live only in a component signal and are dropped on close, thread change, or hide; a null answer (gateway switch off) hides the panel for the page. The panel is hidden for temporary/ZDR threads and Space threads. The spin-off uses the existing new-chat path and sets the composer draft; nothing is created or sent server-side until the person sends. Caveat: the spin-off text enters the composer's normal per-tab draft store like typed text (not for temporary/ZDR chats), so the lead should confirm that matches the no-local-persistence risk line.
- **W5-074-a**: W5-074 part a (F-t3-074): EvaluatePolicyRequest gains target_kind (8) and target_value (9), additive. execution-core derives the target from the tool input: web_fetch and browser.open give the URL host; execute_provider_action mail sends give the first recipient's domain; shell gives the program token and sh code_interpreter gives the first command token. The target is named only while CAPABILITY_PERMISSION_RULES_ENABLED is the literal true (default off; declared for execution-core in the Model Plane compose and the flag registry). On zero-retention or waived runs the kind is sent without its value, so rules that match a value do not apply on those runs.
- **W5-071-c**: W5-071-c: FleetRunConsole is on the BFF. Children come from the thread lineage (listChildRuns) and each child's status from getRun. Per-child Stop and Stop all both call POST /api/v1/orchestration/runs/{run_id}/cancel through cancelRun. Polling runs every 3 s while the parent or a child is unfinished, and stops on unmount. The route is /agents/fleet/:threadId/:runId, still behind VITE_FLEET_CONSOLE=1. The fleet has no budget read yet, so no budget is shown.
- **W5-073-a**: W5-073-a (F-t3-073, first slice): the route note now carries the intent decision's Verevon mode, complexity and a reason token as additive proto fields 7 to 9. All three are closed tokens, so the note stays ZDR-safe. reason is one of mode_cell, budget_tier_shift, budget_cheap_fallback or posture_unknown, derived by intent::Decision::reason(). A constrained posture that changed no model reports mode_cell, matching tier_shifted. A role call carries these fields only when the role table chose a Verevon mode id. Still behind INFERENCE_ROUTE_NOTE (default off), with no new switch and no migration. The Go, Python and Verevon TS bindings need regenerating by the lead.
- **W5-073-b**: 2026-11-10, W5-073-b (routing decision on the answer, F-t3-073). Behind MODEL_GATEWAY_ROUTING_DECISION_ENABLED (model-gateway, default off, literal true; a note needs INFERENCE_ROUTE_NOTE on inference-core), the gateway states the answer's routing decision as closed tokens: mode, complexity, reason, role and role_source, taken from RouteNote fields 7 to 9 (W5-073-a). It appears on the answer's usage_progress frame, its terminal usage frame, the USAGE_ENVELOPE payload and the persisted answer metadata as routing_decision, on the streaming, fallback and ZDR direct paths, and it is written on ZDR turns too because it carries no content. The Details popover shows Mode, Complexity and Reason beside the model; a token the build does not know shows nothing. Status: built and not compiled, not tested, not live-proven. The SSE payload golden still needs the lead's regeneration step (see deviations).
- **W5-073-c**: W5-073-c (F-t3-073 lifecycle successors): /v1/models now carries `successor` (proto ModelInfo field 13) on a deprecated model whose vendor page names a replacement. Only claude-sonnet-4-5 qualifies, with claude-sonnet-5-5 from the model-deprecations page (read 2026-11-10). gemini-2.5-pro and gemini-2.5-flash name no replacement on their vendor pages, so they carry none; D3-077 and D3-080 stay open. The retired-id rewrite moved into model_record.rs (RETIRED table, retired_successor) with behaviour unchanged. Its targets for claude-opus-4-20250514 (claude-opus-5-5) and claude-3-5-haiku-20241022 (claude-haiku-5-5) differ from the vendor-stated replacements (claude-opus-4-8 and claude-haiku-4-5-20251001); the owner must decide before the rewrite changes. Display only: no switch, no routing change, no ZDR path.
- **W5-078-a**: W5-078-a (F-t3-078, role-aware limit notices): built dark behind VITE_CHAT_LIMIT_NOTICES (default off, literal 'true'). limit-notices.ts words budget_exceeded, quota_exceeded, run_ceiling_reached and rate_limited by the active organisation role. Owners and admins get the spend-ceiling route and any server amounts passed in; other roles get a neutral sentence and 'ask an administrator', never a spend or cap figure. rate_limited waits on Retry-After for every role. Wired into the chat stream failure presentation and the Space room budget refusal. Presentation only: nothing is stored, logged or sent, so no ZDR skip is needed. Open: with the flag off, the chat default branch still shows cost-core's budget reason (it carries current spend and the cap) to every role; enabling the flag or fixing the default branch closes it. Chat owners get no billing link because StreamFailureAction has no billing member.
- **W5-079-a**: W5-079-a: the flag registry gains four optional fields. stage is dark, beta or ga, and an absent stage is dark. killSwitch applies to switches only, must default to the safe value (off as implemented), and is never orgOverridable. agentAccess is none or read, and an absent value is none, so no model-facing tool reads a flag by default. orgOverridable applies to switches and choices owned by frontend-plane or model-plane, and a per-org value may narrow or enable a flag only within the build or environment ceiling. Registered as code-sourced entries: MODEL_GATEWAY_REVIEW_RUN_ENABLED (model-plane, review_run.rs) and the frontend switches VITE_AGENT_RESULT_FEEDBACK, VITE_CHAT_READER_POLISH, VITE_CHAT_CHANGED_FILES, VITE_CHAT_DOUBLE_ESCAPE_STOP and VITE_SHELL_TOASTS. Nothing reads the new fields yet. The kill-switch safe value is still an owner decision.
- **W5-079-b**: W5-079-b built, org-core only. Per-org values for the three orgOverridable presentation flags (VITE_AGENT_RESULT_FEEDBACK, VITE_CHAT_CHANGED_FILES, VITE_CHAT_READER_POLISH) live in org_flag_values (migration 032, with org_flag_audit_outbox), behind ORG_FLAGS_ENABLED (strict "true", default off, routes 404). Any active member reads GET /orgs/:id/flags through the gateway (org:read:self). An owner or admin writes PUT /orgs/:id/flags/:name (org:settings:write:self, membership role re-checked, audited like web policy). The internal read GET /internal/orgs/:orgId/flags uses the new scope org:flags:read:any, which no credential holds yet. Presentation only: no security gate reads these values, and nothing content-bearing is stored or audited, so ZDR does not apply to these rows.
- **W5-079-c**: W5-079-c: GET /api/v1/org-flags (session required) reads org-core's internal flags route with the gateway service credential. The gateway cache holds each organisation's answer for 30 seconds, and every answer is no-store. A failed read answers stale, and the SPA then reads every presentation flag as off. Organisation flags are presentation only and are never an authorization input. Operational prerequisite: org-core grants org:flags:read:any to no credential in code, so verevon-gateway needs that grant before ORG_FLAGS_ENABLED is turned on; until then every presentation flag reads off. With org-core's default of false, reader polish is off for each organisation until an owner sets it to true. The SPA reads once per page session, so an organisation switch needs a reload.
